                      PERFORMANCE SPECIFICATION TEST 3                         --.._

            Performance Specification 3 - Performance specifications
            and specification test procedures for monitors of co aiid
       C
            02 from stationary sources.                         2

       =    l. Principle and Applicability.
       =           l.l Principle. Effluent gases a.re continuously
            sampled and a.re analyzed for carbon dioxide or oxygen by a
            continuous monitoring system. Tests of the system a.re
            prefornied during a minimum operating period to determine
            zero dr_ift, calibration drift, and response ti.me character­
            istics.
                  l.2 Applicability. 'rhis performance specification~
            is applicable to evaluation of continuous monitoring
            systems for measurement of carbon dioxide or oxygen.
            These specifications contain test procedures, installation
            requirements, and data computation procedures for
            evaluating the acceptability of the continuous monitoring
            systems subject to approva.l by the ll.CO. . . . . _.
            Sampling may include either extractive or non-extractive
            (in-situ) procedures.
            2. Apparatus.
            or Oxygen.
                                         •
                  2.1 Continuous Monitoring System for Carbon Dioxide         -
                  2. 2 Calibration Gas Matures. Mixture of known
            concentrations of carbon dioxide or oxygen in nitrogen or
           .air. Midrange and 90 percent of span carbon dioxide or
            oxygen concentrations a.re required. The 90 percent of
            span gas mixture is to be used· to set and check the
            analyzer span and is referred to as span gas. For oxygen
            analyzers, if the span is higher than 21 percent o 2 , ambient
            air may be used in ·place of the 90 percent of span calibration
            gas mixture. Triplicate analyses of the gas mixture (expect
            ambient air) shall be perfooned within two weeks prior to
            use using Reference Method 3 or this pa.rt.
                 2.3 Zero Gas. A gas containing less than -100 ppm
           of carbon dioxide or oxygen.
  .
. _.              2. 4 Data Recorder. Analog chart recorder or other
           suitable device with imput voltage range compatible with
           analyzer system output. The resolution of the recorder's
           data. output shall be sufficient to allow completion of the
           test procedures within this specification.

                                    36
                                                                              --
               ·-              3. Definitions.
                                     3.l Continuous Monitoring Systen. The total equipment
                               required for the determination of carbon dioxide or oxygen
                               in a given source effluent. ~he system consists of three
                               major subsystems:
                           •
                                   3.l.l Sampling Interface. That portion of the continuous
                           : monitoring system that performs one or more of the following
                           - operations: delineation, acquisition, transportation, and
                             conditioning of a sample of the source effluent or protection
                             of the analyzer from the hostile aspects of the sam;>le
                             or source environment.
                                     3~1.2 Analyzer-;· -rhat· portion of•· the ·continuous ··
                               monitoring system which senses the pollutant gas and
                               generates a signal output that is a function of the pollutant
                               concentration.
                                     3.1.3 Data Recorder. That port.ion of the continuous
                               monitoring system that provides a permanent record of the
                               output sign.al a te:ms of·concentraticn· units.
                               .     3.2 Span. The value of oxygen or carbon dioxide
                               concentration at vhic:h the continuous monitoring syst~
                               is set that produces the maximum data display output.
         I                     For the purposes of this method, the span shall be set no
                               less than l.5 to 2.5 ti.mes the normal carbon dioxide or

         -                     normal oxygen concentration in the stack gas of the affected
                               facility.
                                      3.3 Midrange. The value of oxygen or carbon dioxide
                               concentration that is representative of the normal conditions
                               in the stack gas of the affected facility at typical operatinq
                               rates.
                                     3.4 Zero Drif~. The change. in the continuous
                               monitoring system output over a stated period of time of
                               normal continuous operation when the carbon dioxide or
                               oxygen concentration at the time for the measurements is
                               zero.
                                     3.S Calibration Drift. The change in the continuous
                               monitoring system output over a stated time period o! nor.nal
                               continuous operation when the carbon dioxide or oxygen
                               continuous monitoring system is measuring the concentration
                           of span gas •
                    ....
                                 3.6 Operational Test Period. A minimum period of
                           time over which the continuous monitoring system is expected
                           to operate within certain performance specifications
                           without unscheduled maintenance, repair, or adjustment.

\   ..,____,



                                                      37
                 3.7 Response Time. '1'he time interval from a step
           change in concentration at the input to the continuous
           monitoring system to the time at which 95 percent of the
           corresponding final value is displayed on the.continuous
           monitoring system data recorder.
       C


           4. Installation Specification.
       ;          Oxygen or·carbon dioxide continuous monitoring
           systems shall be installed at a lopation where measurments
           are directly representative of the total effluent from the
           affected facility or representative of the same effluent
           sampled by a so1 or NOx. continuous monitoring system.
           This requiremen'C. shall De complied with by use of applica.ble
           requirements in Perfo:cmanee Specification 2 of this appendix
           as follows:
                 4.1 Installation.of Oxygen or Carbon Dioxide Continuous
           Monitoring Systems Not Used to Convert Pollutant Dea.
           A sampling location shall be selected in accordance with the
           procedures under paragraphs 4.2.1 or 4.2.2, or Perfo::c:mance
           Specification 2 of this appendix.
                 4.2 Installation of Oxygen er Carbon Dioxide Continuous
           Monitoring Systems Osed to Convert Pollutant Continuous
           Monitoring System Data to Onits of Applicable Standards. The
           diluent continuous monitoring system (oxygen or _carbon           -.
           dioxide) shall be installed at a sampling location where
           measurements that can be made are representative of the
           effluent gases sampled by the· pollutant continuous monito~ing
           system(s). Conformance with this requirement may be accom­
           plished in any of the following ways:
                  4.2.1 -rbe sampling location £or the diluent system
           shall be near the sampling location for the pollutant
           continuous monitoring.system such that the same approximate
           point .(s) (extractive systems) or path (in-situ systems) in
           the cross section is sampled or viewed.

                  4.2.2 The diluent and pollutant continuous monitoring
           systems may be installed at different locations if the
           effluent gases at both sampling locations are nonstratified
           as dete:cmined under paragraphs 4.1 or 4.3, Perfoonance
           Specification 2 of this appendix and there is no in-leakage
           occurring between the two sampling locations. If the
..:.       effluent gases are stratified at either location, the
           procedures under paragraph 4.2.2, Performance Specification
           2 of this appendix shall be used for installating continuous
           monitoring systems at that location.

·-              5. Continuous Monitoring System Performance Specification.
                      The continuous monitoring system shall meet the perfor­
                mance specifications in Table 3-1 to be considered acceptable
                under this method.

                6. Performance Specification Test Procedures.
                      The following test procedures shall be used to
        =      dete:rmi.ne conformance with the requirements of paragraph 4.
            = Due to the wide variation existing in analizer designs and
               principles of operation, these procedures are not·applicable
               to all analyzers. Where this occurs, alternative. procedures,
               subject to the approval of the . APCO         . , may be
             • employed. Any-such alternative .procedures ·must ·.£ulf.ill.
               the same purposes (verify response, drift, and accuracy) as
               the following procedures, and must clearly demonstrate con-
               formance with specifications in table 2-l.             ·
                        6.1 Calibration Check. Establish a calibration
                curve for the continuous monitoring system using zero,
                mid.ra.nge, .. and .span concentration qas.'mixtures. Verify
                that the resultant curve of analyzer reading compared
                with the calibration gas valae is consistent with the
                expected response curve as described by the analyzer manu­
                facturer. If the expected response curve is not produced,
                additional calibration ga.s measurements shall be made,
                or additional s~eps undertaken to verify the accuracy of
(               the response curve of the· analyzer.                         •

-                     6.2 Field Test for Zero Drift and Calibration Drift.
                Install and operate the continuous monitoring system in
                accordance with the manufacturer's written instructions
                and drawings· ·as·-follows; ·

           6.2.l Conditioning Period. Offset the zero setting
                                                                     .-..
     at least 10 percent of span so that negative zero drift
     may be quantified.   Operate the continuous monitoring
     system for an initial 168-bour conditioning period in a
     normal operational manner.
         6.2.2 Operational Test Period. Operate the continuous
 c m:>nitoring system for an additional 168 ?rour period
   maintaining the zero offset. The system shall monitor the
 ; source effluent at all times except when being zeroed,
   calibrated, or backpurged.         ·
            6.2.3 Field Test for Zero Drift and Calibration Drift.
     Determine the values given by zero and midrange gas concen­
      trations at two-hour intervals until 15 sets of data are
     obtained. For non-extractive continuous monitoring systems,
     determine the zero value given by a mechanically·produced
      zero condition or by computing the zero value from upscale
     measurements using calibrated gas cells certified by the
     manufacturer. The midrange checks shall be performed by
     using certified calibration gas cells functionally equivalent
     to less than ~O percent of span. Record these readings on
     the example sheet shown in Figure 2-1. These two-hour
     periods need not be consecutive but may not overlap.
     In-situ CO2 of 02 analyzers which cannot be fitted with a
     calibration gas cell may be calibrated »y alternative
     procedures acceptable to the    AP~       -•  Zero and
     calibration corrections and adjustments are allowed only
                                                                     --
     at 24-hour intervals or at such shorter intervals as the
     :manufacturer's written instructions specify. Automatic
     corrections made by the continuous monitoring system without
     operator intervention or initiation are allowable at any
     time. During the entire 168-hour test peric;,d, record
     the values given by zero and span gas concentrations before
     and after adjustment at 24 hour intervals in the example
     sheet shown in Figure 3-2.
          6.3 Field Test for Response ·Time.
            6.3.l Scope of Test.
            This test shall be accomplished using the continuous
     monitoring system as installed including sample transport
     lines if used. Flow rates, line diameters, pumping rates,
     pressures (do not-allow the pressurized calibration gas
     to change the normal operating pressure in the sample line),
     etd., shall be at the nominal values for normal operation
._.. as specified in t.ae manufacturer• s written instructions.
     If the anal}'%er is used to sample more than one· source
     (stack), this test shall be repeated for each sampling
     point.




                          40
                       6.3.2 Response Time Test Procedure
                       Introduce zero gas into the continuous monitoring
                system sampling interface or as close to the sampling inter­
                face as possible. When the system output reading has
            C
                stabilized, switch quickly to a known concentration of gas
                at 90 percent of span. Record the time from concentration
                switching to 95 percent of final stable response. After
        =       the system response has stabilized at the upper lev~l,
        =       switch quickly to a zero gas. :Record the time from concen­
                tration switching to 95 percent of final stable response.
                Alternatively, for nonextractive continuous monitoring
                systems, the highest available calibration gas concentration
                shall. be .switched .into. and out ·of the sample. path and .. response
                times recorded. Perform this test sequence three
                (3) times. For each test, record the results on t~ data
                sheet shown in Figure 3-3.

                  7.2 Data Analysis and. Reporting.
                  7.2.l Zero Drift (2 hour). Osing the zero concentration
          values measured each two hours during the field test,
          CalC'\.1,late the differences between the consecutive two-hour
          readings expressed in ppm. calculate the mean difference                                                       --
          and the confidence interval using equations 3-l and 3-2.
          Record the sum of the absolute mean value and the confidence
          interval on the data sheet shown in Figure 3-1.
                7.2.2 Zero Drilt (24 hour). tJsing the zero concent­
          ration values measured. every 24 hours during the field    .
          test, calculate the difference between the zero point after
          zero adjustment and the zero value 24 hours.later just
          prior to zero adjustment. Calculate the mean value of
          these points and the confidence interval using equations
          3-1 and 3-2. Record the zero drift (the -sum of the     .
          absolute mean and confidence interval) on the data sheet
          shown in Figure 3-2.

                 7.2.3 Calibration .Drift (2 hour). Osing the calibration
          values obtained at two-hour intervals during the field test,
          calculate the differences between consecutive two-hour readings
 -.
__
          ~ressed as ppm. These values should be corrected for the
          corresponding zero drift during that two-hour period. cal­
          culate these corrected difference values using equations 3-1
          and. 3~2. Do not use the differences between non-consecutive
          readings. Record the sum of the absolute mean and confidence
          interval upon the data sheet shown in Figure 3-1.

                                                                                                                         -
                                                  42
                         7.2.4 calibration Drift (24 hours). Using the
            calibration values measured every 24 hours during the field
            test, calculate the differences between the calibration
            concentration reading after zero and calibration adjustment
            and the calibration concentration reading 24 hours later
          • after zero adjustment but before calibration adjustment.
            Calculate the mean value of these differences and t:he confidence
          = interva.l using equations 3-l and 3-2. Record the sum of the
          : absolute mean and confidence interval on the data sheet shown
            in Figure 3-2.
                    7.2.5 Operational Test Period. During the 168-hour
            perfo:z:mance and operational test period, the continuous
            monitoruig · system shall.. not· receive. any. -cor:ective. maintenance,
            repair, replacem~t, o;- adjustment oth•r than that clearly
            specified as required in the manufacturer's written operation
            and maintenance manuals as routine and expected during a
            one-week period. If the continuous monitoring system operates
            within the specified performance parameters and does not
            require corrective maintenance, repair, replacement or adjustment
            other than.as.specified abov.e during the. 168-hour test pe:iod,
            the operational period will be successfully concluded. Failure
            of the continuous JDOnitoring system tlo meet this requirement
            shall call for a repetition·of the 168 hour test period.
            Portions of the test which were satisfactorily completed need
            not be repeated. Failure to meet any performance specifications
            shall call for a repetition of the one-week perfonnance test
            period and that portion of the testing which is related to the

-           failed specification. All maintenance ant adjustments required
            shall be ·recorded. output readings shall be recorded before
            a.nd after all adjustments.
                 7.2.6 Response·Time. Using the·data developed under·
           paragraph 5.3, calculate the time interval from concentration
           switching to 95 percent to the final stable value for all
           upscale and downscale tests. Report the mean of the three
           upscale test times and the mean of the three downscale test
           times. The two average times should not differ by more than
           15 percent of the slower time. ·Report the slower time as
           the system ·response· time.-·· Record the··results on-Pigure-J-3.

-                         PRO CE DURE FOR CONT INUOUS E!-11 SS IC:J 1101l ITORS


     I. ~op Ii cab i _l_..!...!1.
        &         I.I This procedure Is used to evaluate the accuracy of
    continuous mass emission monitorln~ systems for sulfur dioxide,
    nl~ogen oxides, carbon monoxide, carbon dioxide, oxv~en or any
    ott-rer gaseous compounds whose·monltorlng may be required by the Bay
    Area Air Oual ity Management District.  Both the accuracy of the
    gas species concentration analvzer and the accuracv of the stack
    gas flow rate monitor are tested.
    2. Principle
                 2.1 A (:ontlnuous gas samole, representative of at,.,ospheric
    emissions, is extracted from the source and conditioned to remove
    moisture and particulate material. A smal I portion of the conditioned
    sample is passed through a continuous analyzer sensitive to the
    species of interest. The average concentration of the specie determined
    over    90   minutes is compared with a similar reading determined
    by the continuous emission monitoring system.
                2.2 Carbon dioxide and carbon monoxide are analyzed bv
    non-dispersive infra-red spectoscopy (~DIR). Sulfur dioxide
(
    Is analyzed ~Y ultraviolet (UV) absorption soectroscopv. nxiccs of
    nitrogen arc analyzed as NO by che~i luminescent detection. Oxv~en

-   is analyzed by galvanic measurement at a samole/fuel eel I interface.
                2.3 A pitot tube is used to measure the velocity pressure
    in the stack, from which the stack ~as volumetric flow rate is
    calculated.
    3.   lnterferances
                3.1 The various analytical methods have potential
    interferences as described below.
                  3.1. I Carbon dioxide - methanol, ethanol and water.
    (No i nterterences ezlst with the analyzer mentioned In     1.1. I)
                         3.1.2 Carbon monoxide - evano~en, methyl azide
                         3.1.3 Nitrogen oxides - other nitrogen compounds <except
    ammonia)
       ··"      3 • I • 4 S u I f u r d i ox I de - e I em e n ta I s u I f u r , s u I f u r t r-1 ox i d c ,
    carbon disulfide.
                         3. 1.5 Oxygen - halogens and halogenated compounds.

method herein, the Source Test Section of the BAAPCO should be
consulted or else en elternete method(s) es described In MOP.,
Vol IJ, Source Test Polic~ jnd Procedures she I I be used.

            l.   GROUND LEVEL MONITORING FOR SULFUR DIOXIDE AND HYDROGEN
                 SULFIDE                             ·

-                REF: Regs. l-510
                              9-1-501, 9-1-604'
                              9-2-501, 9-2-601

       •         l.l     General.   Thia section outl1nes the proce~es to

       =         be used tor atmospheric sampling ot·hydrogen sulfide and
       :
                 ault'ur di.oxide.concentrations in order to tultill
                 requirements ot Regulation 9-1-110.2      and Regulation 9-2-301.

                 l.2     Instrumentation.   When required under Regulation
                 9-1-501.:     ~r aegulation 9~2-501,   the person responsible
                 tor emissions shall provide recording instrumentation at
                 not leas than three sites chosen to monitor the ambient
                 air in the area surrounding the emission source and one
                 aeteorologics.J. station to record Wind speed and direction.
(                Additional instruments may be required in specific cases

-                where necessary t~ meet the intent of the appropriate
                 section.    The instruments must be sutticient 1n number
                 to give reasonab~e assurance that any ground level limits
                 exceeding t·hose permitted will be detected.    All analytical
                 instrumentation must be capable of detecting ground level
                 concentrations which exceed the allowable 11.mits.      All
                 instrumentation shall be continuous and equipped With a
                 strip chart recorder.

                 l.2.1    Sultur Dioxide Instrument Specifications.     The
    ....-
                 instruments shall be ot a type which.Will continuously
                 detect and record minute-by-minute fluctuations of
                 concentrations ot aul.1'ur dioxide in th~ range from
                 0.01 ppm (vol) to 1.00 ppm (vol).

                                             1
            1.2.2   Hydrogen Sulfide Instrument Specifications.        The
            recording instruments shall be of a type which will
            continuously detect and record minute-by-minute fluctua­
        C   tions of concentrations or H2S in the range fro~ 0.01 ppm
    =       (vol) to 0.10 ppm ·(vol), or to 0.20 ppm (vol).

            1.2.3   Meteorological Instrument Specifications. Meteoro­
            logica.l instruments she.ll be capable of continuously
            measuring and recording wind direction to within ten
            degrees ot arc, a.nd wind speed to within one mile per hour
            (mph) at wind speeds less than 25 mph and with a threshold
            no greater than 3 mph.      The width of the wind recording
            charts shall be adequate to determine the wind components
            as specified ..

            1.3   Siting.     The instruments shall be installed and
            operated 1n locations which adequately represent ma.ximum
            ground level concentrations ot the measured air pollutants.
            Sites will be chosen to intercept most :frequent ground
            level max:1mum concentrations, but in conformance with
            Regulation 1-52.0.    Proper siting will be taken to require
            ths.t a preponderant downwind exposure over_the calendar
            year be accumulated by the instruments of a given network,
            during their hours or operation.     Ibwnwind exposure exists
            when the mean Wind direction lies 1n the arc within 22.5
.....
            degrees of a direct line from source to monitor.     The
            effective source height and the preva.lent stability class
            associated Wilth the most frequent wind directions are used
            to calculate the most probable distances for ma.x1mum

                                       2
            ground level concentrations.    A station may be placed

-           at or within the property line if the location is otherwise
            acceptable and provi~ed that the person responsible for
            the stations agrees 1n writing that such locati~n shall,
        =   for the purposes of District requirements, be deemed to
        :
            be off the property from which the emissions occur.

            The wind measuring site (or sites) shall be located at the
            source or with the ground level monitors.     In either case,
            they must be high enough and openly exposed so as to be
            tree of interrerence from builcU.ngs, trees, or local
            terrain.   Final approval of the siting of ground level
            monitors and meteorological instrumentation shall be
            with the APCO.
I
            1.4 Ma.intenance.    Regulation 1-510 requires that the
·-          person responsible tor monitoring provide care and main­
            tenance in order to assure that the instruments !'unction
            properly and accurately measure ground level concentrations.
            A record ot consistent instrument downtime may be considered
            :failure to meet this requirement.   The APCO may require
            submission ot maintenance records.

            1.5   Calibration.   Regulation 1-510 requires that the
            person responsible tor monitoring perform periodic calibra-
     __;.   tiona to assure that the instruments provide acceptable
            monitoring ot ground level concentrations.     Addi~ionally,
            a field calibration aha.11 be performed after a.ny relocation
            of an analyzer or major   repair work.   For sult'Ur dioxide the


                                       3
           reference method for calibration is specified in Section
           1.5.6 of this volume; for hydrogen sulfide, in Section
                                                                                   -
           1.5.7.

           As a quality assurance measure, District personnel will
       =
       -   periodically inspect sites, and may perform calibration
           audits on the ground level monitors to determine accuracy.
           Accuracy of a calibration is expressed as the deviation
           between the analyzer response obt&ined under test conditions
           and the results ot the reference procedure when samples of
                                             I

           the s8.llle test gas are taken at approximately the same time.
           Deviation is calculated as a percent of the reference
           results.
                             Analyzer value - Reference value
               ~Deviation•
                             ------------------
                                 Reference value
                                                                           X 100   --.


           'l'he acceptable limit for su1!'ur dioxide analyzer cali­
           brations is± 10~ deviation.    'l'he acceptable limit for.
           hydrogen sulfide analyzer calibrations is+ 15% deviation.
                                                          -
           A record of unacceptable calibrations will be considered
           failure to meet the requirement.       'l'he APCO may require
           submission of calib·ration records.

           1.5.1 Dynamic Calibration Procedure.        A dynamic calibration

__   ...   is a performance test of the entire analyzer system under
           simnlated operating conditions.       The analyzer system
           inclu~es the ana.lyzer; recorder and/or data transmission
           system, and the sampling system with its sample lines and
           sample filters.                                                         -
                                    4
                 The procedure is to allow the analyzer system to sample


    -            a zero air to establish an analyzer zero response
                 (baseline) and to sample test-gas mixtures of known

             C
                 concentration trom a c&libration gas system (see Fig. 1) ·
                 to establish an upscale ~esponse.    A test-gas mixture
             =
                 with concentration approximately equal to midscale of the
                 analyzer range should be used.    The reference methods
                 SUl.tur Dioxide Analytical Precedure 1.5.6, ~drogen SU.lfide
                 Analytical Procedure 1.5.7 contained in this volume are
                 used to verity test-gas concentrations.

                 The test-gas concentration and the analyzer response are
                 used 1n the formula in callbration 1.5 to determine the
                 percent deviation.   The analyzer response is the net
                 reading derived by subtracting the zero response (baseline)
                 from response.to the test gas.    The analyzer should be
                 corrected to make its response conform to_ the known test­
                 gas concentration.   A:t"ter adjustments are made, it is
                 necessary to repeat the sampling process to reestablish
                 both the zert> and upscale a.nalyzer responses.   Linearity
                 and low level response are determined by varying the
                 diluent air calibration gas ratios to provide test gas
                 concentrations throughout the entire range of the analyzer.

                 1.5.2   Zero (Diluent) Air.   Zero air is used to establish
         .
        ._
                 a baseline point ot reference and as a diluent in prepara­
                 tion ot test gas mixtures.    This air must be free or any
                 substances which can alter the test gas mixtures or can

             1.5.3 Calibration Gas System.         A calibration gas system
         =
         :   consists ot a source of diluent air, a source of calibra­
             tion gas, a mixing chamber and a delivery ma.nifold from
             which analyzers and reference sampling trains can sample
             the test gas.   The test gas is prepared by thoroughly
             m:1.xing the c&11brat1on gas     and diluent air 1n various
             known proportions.       The calibration gas can be obtained
             from a permeation device or from a pressurized cylinder
             containing a suitable concentration ot the calibration gas.

             The calibration gas system should be positioned as close
/


-
\
             as is practical to the analyzer and reference sampling
             apparatus to minimize losses.       Sample lines, now metering
             devices, mixing chambers, all other parts of the calibration
             system, as well as the analy:er system should be or
             materials which will not affect the test gas concentration.
             All components uaed 1n the calibration procedure should
             be allowed sufficient time to stabilize before reference
             samples are taken and before analyzer response is determined.

             Pressurized Gas standards
                              .  ·-· ..
                                  .


     .
    ._       Qas standards in pressurized cylinders can be used as a
             gas source to be diluted 1n a calibration gas system or
             used directly when the concentration is within the operating
             range of the analyzer.      Cylinders can be prepared in the

        laboratory or obtained commercially in various concentra­
        tions with or without analysis based on reference methods.
        Such gas standards should be restandardiz:ed against the
        reference method at a minimum of' once every six months.
        Reference samples must be taken each time if a nonstandardized
   =
        gas source is used in the performs.nee of an instrument
        ce.1ibrat1on.

        PP-rmeation Device
        A penaeation device, based on the      dif'f'usion of' a gas through
        a plastic membrane, can be used as a gas source in a
        calibration gas system.    The dif't'Usion rate is a t'Unction of
        temperature and therefore constant temperature must be
        maintained throughout the calibration.       Permeation devices
        are available commercially With or without analysis based              -
        on the reference method.       Certified permeation devices
        should be ste.ndardized grav1metr1cally or against the
        accepted reference method at a minimum or once every six
        months.   Reference samples must be taken each time if' a
        nonstandardized permeation device is used•
                .
        Precautions to be taken with permeation devices include:
             a.   Storage or the device in a dry nitrogen atmosphere,
             preferably between 20 to 25°c.
            b.    Use of' a dry, analyte-f'ree carrier gas •

          1.5.6     Sulfur Dioxide Reference Procedure
          Principle
          The method is based on the absorption of sulfur dioxide
          contained 1n a dilute calibration stream by a solution
          of sodium tetrachloromercurate (TCM).          A stable complex
   =
          is formed, which is then reacted with formaldehyde
          pararosaniline resulting is a colored pararosaniline
          derivative.       The optical density, spectrophotometrically,
          is proportional to the a.mount of su.1fur dioxide initially
          absorbed.
          Reagents
               l.       Absorbing Solution {TCM)
               Dissolve 27.2 g of mercuric chloride and 11.7 g of
               sodium chloride in distilled water and dilute to
               l liter.       The absorbing reagent is stable for 6
                                                                              -
              months.
               2.       Pararosa.niline Hydrochloride Solution
              Dissolve 0.15 g of pararosa.nillne in l liter of
              distilled water c~-=--·:: :'..'."l:!.ng 81 mi of concentrated
                    I

              hydrochloric acid.        Mix well and allow to stand for·
              two days before using.         A new standard curve should
              be prepared for each new batch.          This reagent is
              stable tor 6 months.
              3.        Formaldehyde Solution 0.2"
._....-
              Dilute 0.5 ml ot 37~ formaldehyde solution to 100 ml
              with distilled water.         This solution should be pre­
              pared prior to use.

        A_pl)aratus
              1.      Midget impingers,-such as Ace Glass ·Inc. Cat. No 7531.
              2.      Air pump w1 th tlow control and w1 th a minimum
              capacity ot 2 liters per minute through a midget
   •
              1mp1nger.
   =
   =          3.      Spectrophotometer, suitable tor measurement

       1.5.7 ~drogen Sulfide Reference Procedure
       Princi_ple
       The method is based on the ~bsorption of hydrogen sulfide,
       contained in a dilute calibration stream, by a suspension
       o:t' cadmium hydroxide and arabino    galactan.   The formed
       cadmium sulfide 1s then reacted With N-N D.1.methyl-P-.
       phenylenediamine dihydrochloride and terric chloride to
       make methylene blue.       The methylene blue formed, propor­
       tional to the hydrogen sulfide absorbed, is measured
       spectrophotometrically.
       B!!&j!ienta
....         l.      Absorbing Solution
             Dissolve 2.7 got &nhyd.rcus cadmium sulfate and 10 g
             ot arabino galactan 1n approximately 500 ml ot

      distilled water.      Dissolve 0.3 g of sodium hydroxide
      in approximately 100 ml distilled water.       Mix the
      two solutions slowly while stirring.       Add distilled
      water to make 1 liter or absorbing solution.        The
      absorbing solution is stable for 3 months.
      2.     Test Amine Solution
      Dissolve 0.375 g of N-N dimethyl-P-phenylenedia.mne
      dihydrochloride in 100 ml of 1:1 (v/v) sulfuric acid.
      The solution is stable for 6 months.
     3.      Ferric Chloride Solution
      Dissolve lOOg of ·Fec13 .6 H20 in sufficient water to
     ma.ke 100 ml of solution.      The solution is stable for
     6 months.

                 1.6   Reporting.     Data recorded by the required instrumen­
                 tation shall be exam1n~d at least once every seven days
                 to determine whether the allowable limits have been exceeded,
           •     and to determine whether the instrumentation has operated
           =     properly.   Instrument downtime exceeding a continuous 24
           =
                 hour period and recorded data exceeding allowable limits
                 shall be reported to the APCO Within the next mormal working
                 day following ~'TI!m1nat1on.

                 A summary ot data obtained during each calendar month shall

                 be submitted to the APCO Within 30 days following the end
                 ot the month.      The summary shall include excesses over
                 allowable 11m1ts, mass emission rate trom sources, and
                 notations tor instrument downtime or other loss of data.

                                             16
      2.    ATMOSPHERIC . SAMPLING OF GROUND ~...I, LEAD CONCENTRATIONS

            REF: Regs. l-510, 11-1-501,
                      11-1-662 to 11-1-603
           2.1   General.   This section outlines the procedures to be
           used tor calculation of mass emission limits and tor
=
=          atmospheric sa.mp11ng of ground level l~ad concentrations
           in order to tul!ill requirements of Regulations 11-1-302 and
           11-1-303.
           2.2   Mass Emission Limitations.    Emission limitations
           required to meet Regulation 11-1-302 shall be determined
           by use of formulas 4.1 and 5.13, and figures 3-3 and 3-9,
           in "Workbook of Atmospheric Dispersion Estimates," by
           D. Bruce Turner, Public Health Service Publication No.
           999-AP-26, Revised 1969, pub_lished by the U•.s. Department
           of Health, Education and Welfare.    In using said equations
           and figures, a neutral or "D" stability category.shall be
           assumed, a ·w1nd shall be assumed that remains throughout
           the averaging period directed withi.n a 22.50 sector of the
           compass rose at an average speed of two meters per second,
           and an ambient air temperature of 293°K shall be assumed.

           2.3   Instrumentation.   The person responsible for emissions
           of lead, who has elected to be regulated by Regulation
           ll-l-303 shall provide, install and :maintain not less than
           four directionally controlled high-volume samplers located
.~.        in the area surrounding the source and shall provide at
           least one recording meteorological station equipped to
           record wind speed and wind direction.




                                     17
         2.3.1     Instrument Specification.       High-volume samplers,
         as described in Appendix B, "Reference Method for the
         Determination of Suspended Particulates in the Atmosphere
     4
         (High-Volume Method)", Federal Register, Vol. 36·, No. 84,
     =   Friday, April 30, 1971, shall be fitted with a control
     :
         device which will cause the sampler to operate only during
         those periods when the ambient airflow to the sampler is
         from a specified sector.          Means shall be added to record
         the elapsed time of sampler operation.

         2.3.2     Meteorological Instrument Specifications.      Meteoro­
         logical instruments shall be capable or continuously measuring
         and recordi.ng w1nd direction to Within ten degrees of arc,
         and wind speed to within one mile per hour (mph} at wind
         speeds less than 25 mph, and With a threshold no greater
         than 3 mph.     The width of the Wind recording charts shall
         be adequate to determine the wind components as specified.

         2 •.4   Instrument Siting.   High-volume samplers shall be
         positioned in pairs along vectors ot wind direction passing
         over the source.     The number of wind vectors required for
         monitoring or a site shall be determined from a wind rose
         applicable to the site.      The wind rose shall be as described
         in "some Applications    ot statistics to Meteorology", by
 .
._       Bans Panotsky and Glenn Briar, Mineral Industries Extension
         Service, Pennsylvania State University, pages 15-16, 1958.
         Two such w1nd roses shall be used, one tor the period
         November through April, and one for the period May through
         October.    The requirements or this section apply to each

                                      18
     one independently.

     On each wind rose, a primary vector is drawn in the direction
     of the most frequent winds, and passing through the emission
     point.   This vector is the centerline of a sector of 45°
 =
     of arc (vector direction plus and minus 22.50).    Similar
     treatment of at least one additional vector shall be provided
     to ensure that the sectors as a group encompass at least
     one-half of the exposure +ndicated by the wind rose •
                           .
     A pair of samplers shall be installed along each vector.
     One shall be upwind, and its data will be used 1n determin­
     ing background concentrations; the other shall be downwind,
     and its data will be used in determining source-impacted
     concentrations.    These samplers shall be located as close
     as possible to the points at which.maximum ground level
                                                                     -
     concentrations are predicted by dispersion analysis to occur
     most frequently.   In no case shall the location of the
     sampler be on the property on which the emissions occur
     unless the person responsible for monitoring agrees in
     writing that such location shall, for the purpose of
     District requirements, be deemed off the property.

     Meteorological instruments shall be placed as close to the
     emission source as u practicable while providing a reason­
-.   ably unobstructed exposure to the ~low or ambient air.

     Positioning of all instruments shall be determined in con­
     sultation with the APCO.



                                19
        2.5     Instrument Operation.   The monitoring system shall be
        operated for 24 consecutive hours, at least once every
        three days.     lllring this period, meteorological instruments
        shall be operated continuously; and all samplers 1n the

   =
        system shall be ready to operate, and shall operate wh~n­
   :
        ever the wind direction is within the sector tor which
        controls are set.     Samplers shall be operated, and samples
        obtained, 1n accordance with the specifications as described
        1n Appendix B, "Reference Method tor tne Determination of
        Suspended Particulates in the Atmosphere (High-Volume
        Method)", Federal Register, Vol. 36, No. 84, Friday,
        April 30, 1971.

        2.5.1     Filter Media.   Glass-fiber filters having an initial
        collection efficiency of at least 99 percent for particles
        ot 0.3 µm. diameter are suitable for sample collection.

        2.6 Maintenance.      care and maintenance shall be such that
        the instruments will function properly and record the
        lead concentrations 1n the area.

        2.7   calibration.    calibrations shall be performed to
        assure that the volume or air sampled is accurately known.
        High-volume sampler calibrations shall be performed by
        use ot the Calibration Procedure 1n Appendix B - Reference
.....   Method for the Determination ot Suspended Particulates in
        the Atmosphere {High-Volume Method), Federal Register,
        Vol. 36, No. 84, Friday, April 30, 1971 or any equivalent
        method.



                                   20
        2.8     Analysis.   Lead collected on the filters shall be
        analyzed by Laboratory Procedure No. 4A "Determination
        of Lead Content 1n Atmospheric Particulate Matter".         The
   <·   APCO may require submission o~ tilter samples for com­
   =    parative analysis.

        2.8.1     De.ta Analysis.   Arithmetic means of lead concentra­
        tions shall be computed from all samples obtained during
        a thirty day period.        Two separate arithmetic means shall
        be computed, one for all the background samples and one
        for all samples influenced by the source.       All means
        shall be running means, updated at each subsequent 24-hour
        sample to include data for a thirty day period.

        2.9   Reporting.    A   summary of data obtained from sampling
        and analysis and from the meteorological instrumentation,
        shall be submitted to the APCO within 30 days following
        the end of the month.
